## Amos 9:2 (#2) (id: en_tn_AMO_vr1s)

אִם־יַחְתְּר֣וּ בִ⁠שְׁא֔וֹל מִ⁠שָּׁ֖ם יָדִ֣⁠י תִקָּחֵ֑⁠ם

**"If they dig into Sheol, from there my hand will take them"**

Here and in the next verse, Amos lists a series of an unreal, hypothetical situations that make the point that no matter where the people would try to hide themselves in order to escape Yahweh’s judgment, Yahweh will still find and punish them. Use a natural method in your language for introducing an imaginary situation. Alternate translation: \[Even if they could dig way down into Sheol, from there my hand would take them]

See: Hypothetical Situations

* **Associated Passages:** Amos 9:2
* **Associated Articles:** Hypothetical Situations (ID: `33282@UWTranslationNotes`)

## Amos 9:3 (#2) (id: en_tn_AMO_n1cc)

וְ⁠אִם־יִסָּ֨תְר֜וּ מִ⁠נֶּ֤גֶד עֵינַ⁠י֙ בְּ⁠קַרְקַ֣ע הַ⁠יָּ֔ם

**"And if they are hidden from before my eyes in the floor of the sea"**

The expression **are hidden** is a passive verbal form. If it would be unnatural to use that form, you could express the idea in active form or in another way that is natural in your language. Alternate translation: \[And if they hide themselves from before my eyes in the floor of the sea]

See: Active or Passive

## Amos 9:4 (#1) (id: en_tn_AMO_b3bo)

וְ⁠אִם־יֵלְכ֤וּ בַ⁠שְּׁבִי֙ לִ⁠פְנֵ֣י אֹֽיבֵי⁠הֶ֔ם

**"And if they go in captivity to the face of their enemies"**

If your language does not use an abstract noun for the idea of **captivity**, you could express the same idea in another way. Alternate translation: \[And if their enemies capture them and carry them off]

See: Abstract Nouns

## Amos 9:4 (#2) (id: en_tn_AMO_bpsa)

לִ⁠פְנֵ֣י אֹֽיבֵי⁠הֶ֔ם

**"to the face of their enemies"**

Here, **to the face of** represents being in the presence of or in front of those enemies. If it would be helpful in your language, you could use an equivalent expression or plain language. Alternate translation: \[in front of their enemies]

* **Associated Passages:** Amos 9:4
* **Associated Articles:** Metonymy (ID: `33293@UWTranslationNotes`)


## Amos 9:4 (#3) (id: en_tn_AMO_a575)

אֲצַוֶּ֥ה אֶת־הַ⁠חֶ֖רֶב וַ⁠הֲרָגָ֑תַ⁠ם

**"I will command the sword and it will kill them"**

Here, **the sword** represents the enemies who would use swords to kill them. If it would be helpful in your language, you could use an equivalent expression or plain language. Alternate translation: \[I will command their enemies and they will kill them]

See: Metonymy

* **Associated Passages:** Amos 9:4
* **Associated Articles:** Metonymy (ID: `33293@UWTranslationNotes`)


## Amos 9:4 (#4) (id: en_tn_AMO_gqqd)

וְ⁠שַׂמְתִּ֨י עֵינִ֧⁠י עֲלֵי⁠הֶ֛ם

**"And I will set my eye on them"**

The Lord is using **I will set my eye on them** as a common expression of the culture to mean “I will watch them closely,” usually in a positive sense of caring for them but here it is reversed in that it means he is watching them in order to harm them and not to help them. If this phrase does not have that meaning for your readers, you could use a comparable expression from your language that does have that meaning, or you could state the meaning plainly. Alternate translation: \[And I will watch them closely]

See: Idiom

* **Associated Passages:** Amos 9:4
* **Associated Articles:** Idiom (ID: `33283@UWTranslationNotes`)


## Amos 9:4 (#5) (id: en_tn_AMO_ccyq)

לְ⁠רָעָ֖ה וְ⁠לֹ֥א לְ⁠טוֹבָֽה

**"for evil and not for good"**

If your language does not use abstract nouns for the ideas of **evil** and **good**, you could express the same ideas in other ways. Alternate translation: \[to punish them and not to benefit them]

See: Abstract Nouns

* **Associated Passages:** Amos 9:4
* **Associated Articles:** Abstract Nouns (ID: `33257@UWTranslationNotes`)

Amos is using the possessive form **the Lord Yahweh of Armies** to describe Yahweh as the one who commands the armies of heaven. It may be helpful to clarify this for your readers. Alternate translation: \[And the Lord Yahweh, who commands the armies of heaven]

## Amos 9:6 (#1) (id: en_tn_AMO_zfbj)

מעלות⁠ו

**"his stairs"**

The word translated as **stairs** represents the standard written Hebrew text as we have it today. Some scholars have proposed, however, that a scribe may have added an extra letter to this word. Without the extra letter, it would say “upper rooms,” which is how some modern versions translate. You may want to follow the major translation in your area. Alternate translation: \[his upper rooms]

See: Alternative Readings

* **Associated Passages:** Amos 9:6

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Amos 9:6 (#2) (id: en_tn_AMO_xdb2)

וַ⁠אֲגֻדָּת֖⁠וֹ

**"and his vault"**

A **vault** is a kind of arched or domed roof. Amos uses the term to describe the sky as a dome stretched over the earth. If your readers would not be familiar with what a vault is, you could use the name of a similar thing that your readers would recognize, or you could use a more general term. Alternate translation: \[and the sky]

See: Translate Unknowns

* **Associated Passages:** Amos 9:6
* **Associated Articles:** Translate Unknowns (ID: `33408@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Amos 9:7 (#1) (id: en_tn_AMO_rxec)

הֲ⁠ל֣וֹא כִ⁠בְנֵי֩ כֻשִׁיִּ֨ים אַתֶּ֥ם לִ֛⁠י בְּנֵ֥י יִשְׂרָאֵ֖ל

**"Are you not like the sons of the Cushites to me, sons of Israel"**

Yahweh is using the question form to insist that the Israelites are no longer more privileged in his sight than any of these other people groups because they have broken their special covenant with him. If a speaker of your language would not use the question form for that purpose, you could translate this as a statement or as an exclamation. Alternate translation: \[You are just like the sons of the Cushites to me, sons of Israel!]

See: Rhetorical Question

* **Associated Passages:** Amos 9:7
* **Associated Articles:** Rhetorical Question (ID: `33310@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Philistines (ID: `group:Philistine`); Kir (ID: `place:Kir`); Cushites (ID: `group:Cush`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Cush (ID: `place:Cush.2`); Philistia (ID: `place:Philistine`); Crete (ID: `place:Crete`); Syrians (ID: `group:Syria`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Egypt (ID: `place:Egypt`)

## Amos 9:7 (#2) (id: en_tn_AMO_jju8)

כִ⁠בְנֵי֩ כֻשִׁיִּ֨ים \& לִ֛⁠י בְּנֵ֥י יִשְׂרָאֵ֖ל

**"like the sons of the Cushites to me, sons of Israel"**

Yahweh is speaking as if the people of the nation of Cush, which is modern day Ethiopia, were actually **the sons** of Cush who was the son of Ham, and the people of Israel where the actual **sons** of the patriarch, Israel. Alternate translation: \[like the Cushite people to me, you people of Israel]

See: Metaphor

* **Associated Passages:** Amos 9:7
* **Associated Articles:** Metaphor (ID: `33292@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Philistines (ID: `group:Philistine`); Kir (ID: `place:Kir`); Cushites (ID: `group:Cush`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Cush (ID: `place:Cush.2`); Philistia (ID: `place:Philistine`); Crete (ID: `place:Crete`); Syrians (ID: `group:Syria`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Egypt (ID: `place:Egypt`)

## Amos 9:7 (#3) (id: en_tn_AMO_pad9)

נְאֻם־יְהוָ֑ה

**"the declaration of Yahweh"**

Amos, who is conveying Yahweh’s words, is interjecting **the declaration of Yahweh** to emphasize that what is being said is a direct quotation from Yahweh and comes with Yahweh’s authority. In your translation, use a natural way of indicating direct quotations in your language.

See: Quotations and Quote Margins

* **Associated Passages:** Amos 9:7
* **Associated Articles:** Quotations and Quote Margins (ID: `33426@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Philistines (ID: `group:Philistine`); Kir (ID: `place:Kir`); Cushites (ID: `group:Cush`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Cush (ID: `place:Cush.2`); Philistia (ID: `place:Philistine`); Crete (ID: `place:Crete`); Syrians (ID: `group:Syria`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Egypt (ID: `place:Egypt`)

## Amos 9:7 (#4) (id: en_tn_AMO_nxtr)

הֲ⁠ל֣וֹא אֶת־יִשְׂרָאֵ֗ל הֶעֱלֵ֨יתִי֙ מֵ⁠אֶ֣רֶץ מִצְרַ֔יִם וּ⁠פְלִשְׁתִּיִּ֥ים מִ⁠כַּפְתּ֖וֹר וַ⁠אֲרָ֥ם מִ⁠קִּֽיר

**"Did I not bring Israel up from the land of Egypt, and the Philistines from Caphtor, and Aram from Kir"**

Yahweh is using the question form to remind the Israelites that he is the one who controls where nations come and go just as he brought Israel out of Egypt. If a speaker of your language would not use the question form for that purpose, you could translate this as a statement or as an exclamation. Alternate translation: \[Not only did I bring Israel up from the land of Egypt, but I also brought the Philistines from Caphtor, and the Arameans from Kir.]

See: Rhetorical Question

* **Associated Passages:** Amos 9:7
* **Associated Articles:** Rhetorical Question (ID: `33310@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Philistines (ID: `group:Philistine`); Kir (ID: `place:Kir`); Cushites (ID: `group:Cush`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Cush (ID: `place:Cush.2`); Philistia (ID: `place:Philistine`); Crete (ID: `place:Crete`); Syrians (ID: `group:Syria`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Egypt (ID: `place:Egypt`)

**"the eyes of the Lord Yahweh are on the sinful kingdom"**

פְּנֵ֣י הָ⁠אֲדָמָ֑ה

**"the face of the ground"**

Yahweh is using **the face of the ground** as a common expression of the culture to mean “the surface of the earth” or “the whole earth.” If this phrase does not have that meaning for your readers, you could use a comparable expression from your language that does have that meaning, or you could state the meaning plainly. ## Amos 9:8 (#5) (id: en_tn_AMO_hlt7)

אֶ֗פֶס כִּ֠י לֹ֣א הַשְׁמֵ֥יד אַשְׁמִ֛יד אֶת־בֵּ֥ית יַעֲקֹ֖ב

**"Nevertheless, destroying, I will not destroy the house of Jacob"**

Yahweh uses the word **Nevertheless** here to indicate a contrast between the destruction of the sinful northern kingdom and his promise to preserve a remnant from the descendants of Jacob. The fact that he will preserve a remnant is evident in the context of the next verse. In your translation, indicate this contrast in a way that is natural in your language. Alternate translation: \[But destroying, I will not destroy the entire house of Jacob]

See: Connect — Contrast Relationship

* **Associated Passages:** Amos 9:8
* **Associated Articles:** Connect — Contrast Relationship (ID: `33329@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** To Sin (ID: `keyterm:Sin`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Amos 9:8 (#6) (id: en_tn_AMO_v42r)

לֹ֣א הַשְׁמֵ֥יד אַשְׁמִ֛יד

**"destroying, I will not destroy"**

Yahweh is repeating forms of the verb **destroy** in order to intensify his expression of the idea that it conveys. If your language can repeat words for intensification, it would be appropriate to do that here in your translation. If not, your language may have another way of expressing the intensification. Alternate translation: \[I will certainly not completely destroy]

See: Reduplication

* **Associated Passages:** Amos 9:8

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** To Sin (ID: `keyterm:Sin`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Amos 9:8 (#8) (id: en_tn_AMO_zspo)

אֶת־בֵּ֥ית יַעֲקֹ֖ב

**"the house of Jacob"**

Here, **house** means all the people descended from a particular person. It envisions them as if they were one household living together. So **the house of Jacob** figuratively means all the people descended from the patriarch Jacob, who was also known as Israel. Alternate translation: \[all those descended from Jacob]

הִנֵּ֤ה

**"behold"**

## Amos 9:9 (#2) (id: en_tn_AMO_chs2)

אָֽנֹכִי֙ מְצַוֶּ֔ה

**"I am commanding"**

Yahweh is leaving out some of the words that in many languages a sentence would need in order to be complete. You can supply these words from the context if that would be clearer in your language. Alternate translation: \[I am commanding that this happen]

See: Ellipsis

* **Associated Passages:** Amos 9:9
* **Associated Articles:** Ellipsis (ID: `33267@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Sieve (ID: `realia:Sieve`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`)

## Amos 9:9 (#3) (id: en_tn_AMO_e1ja)

וַ⁠הֲנִע֥וֹתִי בְ⁠כָֽל־הַ⁠גּוֹיִ֖ם אֶת־בֵּ֣ית יִשְׂרָאֵ֑ל כַּ⁠אֲשֶׁ֤ר יִנּ֨וֹעַ֙ בַּ⁠כְּבָרָ֔ה

**"and I will shake the house of Israel among all of the nations, just as it is shaken in the sieve"**

The point of this comparison is that just as grain **is shaken in the sieve** in order to sort it, so Yahweh will shake **the house of Israel among all of the nations**, which included exile and dispersement among other nations, in order to separate the faithful people from the unfaithful ones. If it would be helpful in your language, you could make this point explicitly. Alternate translation: \[and I will shake the house of Israel among all of the nations to separate the faithful from the unfaithful, just as grain is shaken in a sieve to separate it from stones]

See: Simile

* **Associated Passages:** Amos 9:9
* **Associated Articles:** Simile (ID: `33314@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Sieve (ID: `realia:Sieve`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`)

## Amos 9:9 (#4) (id: en_tn_AMO_jb9a)

אֶת־בֵּ֣ית יִשְׂרָאֵ֑ל

**"the house of Israel"**

Yahweh is speaking of all the people descended from the patriarch Jacob, who was also known as Israel, as if they were one **house**. Alternate translation: \[the people of Israel]

See: Metaphor

* **Associated Passages:** Amos 9:9
* **Associated Articles:** Metaphor (ID: `33292@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Sieve (ID: `realia:Sieve`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`)

## Amos 9:9 (#5) (id: en_tn_AMO_g34z)

כַּ⁠אֲשֶׁ֤ר יִנּ֨וֹעַ֙ בַּ⁠כְּבָרָ֔ה

**"just as it is shaken in the sieve"**

The expression **is shaken** is a passive verbal form. If it would be unnatural to use that form, you could express the idea in active form or in another way that is natural in your language. Alternate translation: \[just as someone shakes grain in a sieve]

See: Active or Passive

* **Associated Passages:** Amos 9:9
* **Associated Articles:** Active or Passive (ID: `33258@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Sieve (ID: `realia:Sieve`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`)

## Amos 9:9 (#6) (id: en_tn_AMO_i3wt)

בַּ⁠כְּבָרָ֔ה

**"in the sieve"**

A **sieve** is a kind of container with holes in the bottom that a farmer shakes in order to separate grain from stones and dirt. If your readers would not be familiar with what a sieve is, you could use the name of a similar thing that your readers would recognize, or you could use a more general term. Alternate translation: \[in a screen for sifting grain]

See: Translate Unknowns

* **Associated Passages:** Amos 9:9
* **Associated Articles:** Translate Unknowns (ID: `33408@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Sieve (ID: `realia:Sieve`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`)

## Amos 9:9 (#7) (id: en_tn_AMO_crw4)

וְ⁠לֹֽא־יִפּ֥וֹל צְר֖וֹר אָֽרֶץ

**"and a pebble will not fall to the earth"**

The meaning of the word translated as **pebble** could also be translated as “small grain,” and thus it is uncertain whether the grain stays in the sieve or passes through. In either case, the meaning of the comparison is the same; God is using the nations as a sieve to sort out the people of Israel into those who are faithful to him (the grain) and those who are not (the stones). You may want to choose how you translate this comparison depending on whether the grain in your area is bigger or smaller than the holes in a sieve. To summarize, this word could mean: (1\) a small stone. In this case, the sieve is retaining the stones and letting the grain fall through, as in the ULT and UST. (2\) a small piece of something, such as grain. In this case, the sieve is retaining the grain and letting the small stones fall through. Alternate translation: \[and a small grain will not fall to the earth]

See: Assumed Knowledge and Implicit Information

* **Associated Passages:** Amos 9:9
* **Associated Articles:** Assumed Knowledge and Implicit Information (ID: `33273@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Sieve (ID: `realia:Sieve`); Israel (ID: `person:Israel`)

## Amos 9:10 (#1) (id: en_tn_AMO_nz71)

הָ⁠אֹמְרִ֗ים לֹֽא־תַגִּ֧ישׁ וְ⁠תַקְדִּ֛ים בַּעֲדֵ֖י⁠נוּ הָ⁠רָעָֽה

**"the ones saying, ‘The evil will not approach or reach as far as us"**

If it would be clearer in your language, you could translate this so that there is not a quotation within a quotation. Alternate translation: \[the ones saying that the evil will not approach or reach as far as them]

See: Quotes within Quotes

* **Associated Passages:** Amos 9:10
* **Associated Articles:** Quotes within Quotes (ID: `33307@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** To Sin (ID: `keyterm:Sin`)

## Amos 9:10 (#2) (id: en_tn_AMO_ktel)

לֹֽא־תַגִּ֧ישׁ וְ⁠תַקְדִּ֛ים בַּעֲדֵ֖י⁠נוּ הָ⁠רָעָֽה

**"The evil will not approach or reach as far as us"**

The sinners are speaking of suffering harm as if **The evil** were a living thing that could **approach** or **reach** them. If it would be helpful in your language, you could state the meaning plainly. Alternate translation: \[We will not suffer any harm]

See: Personification

* **Associated Passages:** Amos 9:10
* **Associated Articles:** Personification (ID: `33302@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** To Sin (ID: `keyterm:Sin`)

## Amos 9:10 (#3) (id: en_tn_AMO_rndc)

הָ⁠רָעָֽה

**"The evil"**

If your language does not use an abstract noun for the idea of **evil**, you could express the same idea in another way. Alternate translation: \[Bad things]

See: Abstract Nouns

* **Associated Passages:** Amos 9:10
* **Associated Articles:** Abstract Nouns (ID: `33257@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** To Sin (ID: `keyterm:Sin`)

## Amos 9:11 (#1) (id: en_tn_AMO_tvpz)

בַּ⁠יּ֣וֹם הַ⁠ה֔וּא

**"In that day"**

Yahweh is using the phrase translated as **In that day** to introduce a time of restoration. Unlike this phrase elsewhere in the book, it does not seem to indicate the time of punishment as in the “day of Yahweh” but the restoration that can occur because it is now possible.

See: Introduction of a New Event

* **Associated Passages:** Amos 9:11
* **Associated Articles:** Introduction of a New Event (ID: `33419@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** David (ID: `person:David`)

## Amos 9:11 (#2) (id: en_tn_AMO_lxmy)

אָקִ֛ים אֶת־סֻכַּ֥ת דָּוִ֖יד הַ⁠נֹּפֶ֑לֶת

**"I will raise up the fallen booth of David"**

Yahweh is speaking as if the dynasty or kingdom of David were a **booth**, a temporary shelter, that had collapsed and that he would restore or **raise up** again. He goes on to speak of repairing its **breaches** and rebuilding its **ruins**. Your language may have a comparable expression that you can use in your translation, or you could state the meaning plainly. Alternate translation: \[I will restore the fallen dynasty of David]

See: Metaphor

* **Associated Passages:** Amos 9:11
* **Associated Articles:** Metaphor (ID: `33292@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** David (ID: `person:David`)

## Amos 9:11 (#3) (id: en_tn_AMO_yq39)

אֶת־פִּרְצֵי⁠הֶ֗ן

**"their breaches"**

The pronoun **their** probably refers to the cities of David’s kingdom. It may be clearer to use a singular pronoun to refer to the kingdom itself. Alternate translation: \[its breaches]

See: Pronouns — When to Use Them

* **Associated Passages:** Amos 9:11
* **Associated Articles:** Pronouns — When to Use Them (ID: `33424@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** David (ID: `person:David`)

## Amos 9:12 (#1) (id: en_tn_AMO_r42i)

אֲשֶׁר־נִקְרָ֥א שְׁמִ֖⁠י עֲלֵי⁠הֶ֑ם

**"whom my name is called over them"**

Yahweh is using **my name is called over them** as a common expression of the culture to mean “they belong to me.” If this phrase does not have that meaning for your readers, you could use a comparable expression from your language that does have that meaning, or you could state the meaning plainly. Alternate translation: \[that belong to me]

See: Idiom

* **Associated Passages:** Amos 9:12
* **Associated Articles:** Idiom (ID: `33283@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Edom (ID: `place:Edom`); Dispossess (ID: `keyterm:Dispossess`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Amos 9:12 (#2) (id: en_tn_AMO_p3mj)

נְאֻם־יְהוָ֖ה עֹ֥שֶׂה זֹּֽאת

**"the declaration of Yahweh, the one doing this"**

Amos, who is conveying Yahweh’s words, is once again interjecting **the declaration of Yahweh** and this time adding **the one doing this** to emphasize that what has been said will surely happen as it is a direct quotation from Yahweh and comes with Yahweh’s authority. In your translation, use a natural way of indicating direct quotations in your language.

See: Quotations and Quote Margins

* **Associated Passages:** Amos 9:12
* **Associated Articles:** Quotations and Quote Margins (ID: `33426@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Edom (ID: `place:Edom`); Dispossess (ID: `keyterm:Dispossess`); LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Amos 9:13 (#1) (id: en_tn_AMO_jfv9)

הִנֵּ֨ה יָמִ֤ים בָּאִים֙

**"Behold, days are coming"**

Yahweh is using the term **Behold** to focus his listeners’ attention on what he is about to say about **the days** or the specific time that is coming. Your language may have a comparable expression that you can use in your translation. Alternate translation: \[Pay attention, for the time is coming]

See: Foreground Information

* **Associated Passages:** Amos 9:13

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Barley (ID: `flora:Barley`); Must (ID: `realia:Must`); Vine (ID: `flora:Vine`); Mattock (ID: `realia:Mattock`); Coriander (ID: `flora:Coriander`)

## Amos 9:13 (#2) (id: en_tn_AMO_z394)

נְאֻם־יְהוָ֔ה

**"the declaration of Yahweh"**

Amos, who is conveying Yahweh’s words, is once again interjecting **the declaration of Yahweh** to emphasize that what is about to be said is a direct quotation from Yahweh and comes with Yahweh’s authority. In your translation, use a natural way of indicating direct quotations in your language.

See: Quotations and Quote Margins

* **Associated Passages:** Amos 9:13
* **Associated Articles:** Quotations and Quote Margins (ID: `33426@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Barley (ID: `flora:Barley`); Must (ID: `realia:Must`); Vine (ID: `flora:Vine`); Mattock (ID: `realia:Mattock`); Coriander (ID: `flora:Coriander`)

## Amos 9:13 (#3) (id: en_tn_AMO_occ8)

וְ⁠נִגַּ֤שׁ חוֹרֵשׁ֙ בַּ⁠קֹּצֵ֔ר וְ⁠דֹרֵ֥ךְ עֲנָבִ֖ים בְּ⁠מֹשֵׁ֣ךְ הַ⁠זָּ֑רַע

**"and the plower will approach the reaper, and the treader of grapes to the sower of the seed"**

The implication is that the harvests will be so large that the plowing and planting seasons will begin before the reaping and the treading of grapes are finished. This was one of the covenant promises mentioned in Leviticus 26:5\. You could include this information in a footnote if that would be helpful to your readers.

See: Assumed Knowledge and Implicit Information

* **Associated Passages:** Amos 9:13
* **Associated Articles:** Assumed Knowledge and Implicit Information (ID: `33273@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Barley (ID: `flora:Barley`); Must (ID: `realia:Must`); Vine (ID: `flora:Vine`); Mattock (ID: `realia:Mattock`); Coriander (ID: `flora:Coriander`)

## Amos 9:13 (#4) (id: en_tn_AMO_d478)

וְ⁠דֹרֵ֥ךְ עֲנָבִ֖ים בְּ⁠מֹשֵׁ֣ךְ הַ⁠זָּ֑רַע

**"and the treader of grapes to the sower of the seed"**

Yahweh is leaving out some of the words that in many languages a sentence would need in order to be complete. You can supply these words from the context if that would be clearer in your language. Alternate translation: \[and the treader of grapes will approach the sower of the seed]

See: Ellipsis

* **Associated Passages:** Amos 9:13
* **Associated Articles:** Ellipsis (ID: `33267@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Barley (ID: `flora:Barley`); Must (ID: `realia:Must`); Vine (ID: `flora:Vine`); Mattock (ID: `realia:Mattock`); Coriander (ID: `flora:Coriander`)

## Amos 9:13 (#5) (id: en_tn_AMO_rbyr)

וְ⁠הִטִּ֤יפוּ הֶֽ⁠הָרִים֙ עָסִ֔יס וְ⁠כָל־הַ⁠גְּבָע֖וֹת תִּתְמוֹגַֽגְנָה

**"and the mountains will drip sweet wine, and all of the hills will melt"**

Yahweh is speaking as if **the mountains** and **the hills** themselves were producing **sweet wine** in such quantity that it flowed down their slopes. Your language may have a comparable expression that you can use in your translation, or you could state the meaning plainly. Alternate translation: \[and the vineyards on the mountains and the hills will produce great amounts of sweet wine]

See: Metaphor

* **Associated Passages:** Amos 9:13
* **Associated Articles:** Metaphor (ID: `33292@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`); Barley (ID: `flora:Barley`); Must (ID: `realia:Must`); Vine (ID: `flora:Vine`); Mattock (ID: `realia:Mattock`); Coriander (ID: `flora:Coriander`)

## Amos 9:14 (#1) (id: en_tn_AMO_ohl0)

וְ⁠שַׁבְתִּי֮ אֶת־שְׁב֣וּת

**"And I will restore the restoration of"**

Yahweh is using a construction in which a verb and its object come from the same root. You may be able to use the same construction in your language to express the meaning here. Alternatively, your language may have another way of saying this. This could mean: (1\) that he will restore the prosperity of his people as it was before they were exiled, as in the UST, or (2\) that he will bring his exiled people back to their land. Alternate translation: \[And I will restore from their exile]

See: Poetry

* **Associated Passages:** Amos 9:14
* **Associated Articles:** Poetry (ID: `33422@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** Israel (ID: `person:Israel`); Wine (ID: `realia:Wine`); Vine (ID: `flora:Vine`)

## Amos 9:15 (#1) (id: en_tn_AMO_l004)

וּ⁠נְטַעְתִּ֖י⁠ם עַל־אַדְמָתָ֑⁠ם וְ⁠לֹ֨א יִנָּתְשׁ֜וּ ע֗וֹד מֵ⁠עַ֤ל אַדְמָתָ⁠ם֙

**"And I will plant them upon their ground, and they will not be uprooted again from upon their ground"**

Yahweh is speaking of restoring the Israelites to their land as if they were plants that he would **plant** in the ground that would never be **uprooted** again. Your language may have a comparable expression that you can use in your translation, or you could state the meaning plainly. Alternate translation: \[And I will settle them permanently in their land, and they will not be driven out again from upon the land]

See: Metaphor

* **Associated Passages:** Amos 9:15
* **Associated Articles:** Metaphor (ID: `33292@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Amos 9:15 (#2) (id: en_tn_AMO_nuuz)

וְ⁠לֹ֨א יִנָּתְשׁ֜וּ ע֗וֹד מֵ⁠עַ֤ל אַדְמָתָ⁠ם֙

**"and they will not be uprooted again from upon their ground"**

The expression **be uprooted** is a passive verbal form. If it would be unnatural to use that form, you could express the idea in active form or in another way that is natural in your language. Alternate translation: \[and no one will uproot them again from upon their ground]

See: Active or Passive

* **Associated Passages:** Amos 9:15
* **Associated Articles:** Active or Passive (ID: `33258@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)

## Amos 9:15 (#3) (id: en_tn_AMO_n6n8)

אֱלֹהֶֽי⁠ךָ

**"your God"**

Amos is not using this possessive form **your God** to indicate that the Israelites possess or control Yahweh. Rather, he is using these words to remind them that Yahweh is the God who chose them and whom they should worship. Alternate translation: \[the God whom you worship]

See: Possession

* **Associated Passages:** Amos 9:15
* **Associated Articles:** Possession (ID: `33303@UWTranslationNotes`)

* **Associated ACAI Concepts:** LORD (ID: `deity:Lord`)
